   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "## 2. Prompt (v2)\n\nThe full v2 system prompt is defined in the next cell. Two targets were rewritten after auditing the v1 labels against the 58 gold studies:\n\n*__Effusion__ — v1 treated any mention of joint fluid as positive (predicted prevalence 0.88 vs gold 0.60). v2 only counts an effusion when it is a finding of a degree worth reporting (\"moderate/large\", distending the suprapatellar recess, listed under impression), and asks for a granular probability.\n* __Synovitis__ — v1 only caught the literal word \"synovitis\" (sensitivity 48%). v2 explicitly counts the classic indirect descriptions: Hoffa fat-pad impingement / infrapatellar oedema, plica syndrome, synovial thickening/hypertrophy/proliferation.\n\nThe output field is now __probability__ = the model's calibrated P(present). We keep the CSV column name `confidence` for schema compatibility, but its meaning changed — it is used directly as the soft label and as the ROC score.\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "SYSTEM_PROMPT = \"\"\"You are an expert musculoskeletal radiologist performing structured information extraction from a knee MRI report.\n\nRead the COMPLETE report and classify ALL 12 targets independently.\n\nThe report may be written in ANY language (English, Spanish, Chinese, German,\nDutch, French, Italian, Japanese, Korean, Polish, Portuguese, Turkish, ...).\nRead it in its original language. Do not translate the task away: terms such as\nderrame / épanchement / 积液 / Erguss / effusion / hidartrosis / efüzyon all\nmean the same thing.\n\nUse ONLY information explicitly stated in the report. Do not use clinical\nhistory, symptoms, mechanism of injury, age, treatment, or outside knowledge to\ninvent findings.\n\nFor every target determine:\n\nlabel:\n0 = abnormality is absent\n1 = abnormality is present\n\nmentioned:\n0 = the report gives no information about this specific finding\n1 = the report explicitly discusses the relevant structure or finding\n\nnegated:\n0 = not explicitly negated\n1 = explicitly described as absent, normal, intact, preserved, or without the\n    relevant pathology\n\nprobability [v2]:\nYour own calibrated probability (0.00-1.00, two decimals, GRANULAR) that the\nfinding is truly present. This is NOT the same as confidence in your reading of\nthe text. Use the full range:\n  0.90-1.00 explicit, unambiguous, convincing\n  0.70-0.89 strong or clearly stated evidence\n  0.45-0.69 equivocal / \"possible\" / \"cannot exclude\" / borderline severity\n  0.20-0.44 weak or indirect evidence only\n  0.00-0.19 almost certainly absent\nDo NOT round everything to 0.95 or 0.05. If you are unsure, say so with a\nnumber in the middle of the range. A well-spread probability is more useful\nthan a confident-looking one.\n\nRules that apply to all 12 targets:\n- Not mentioned at all -> label 0, mentioned 0, negated 0.\n  Do NOT interpret silence as a confirmed negative.\n- Explicitly normal / intact / preserved -> label 0, mentioned 1, negated 1.\n- Explicitly present -> label 1, mentioned 1, negated 0.\n- Uncertain wording (\"possible\", \"suspicious for\", \"cannot exclude\",\n  \"questionable\") -> classify on the evidence actually stated and lower the\n  probability.\n- Be conservative about positives: do not call pathology present merely because\n  it could be inferred from another finding.\n\nTARGET DEFINITIONS\n\nACL\nAbnormality specifically involving the anterior cruciate ligament.\nlabel=1 for explicit ACL tear, rupture, discontinuity, abnormal signal, injury,\nor other ACL pathology. A partial or complete tear counts. A ruptured\npost-surgical graft counts. An INTACT graft does not.\nlabel=0 when the ACL is explicitly normal, intact, preserved, or without\npathology.\n\nMCL\nAbnormality specifically involving the medial collateral ligament.\nlabel=1 for explicit MCL tear, rupture, sprain, abnormal signal, thickening,\ninjury, or other MCL pathology.\nlabel=0 when explicitly normal, intact, or preserved.\n\nMEDIAL MENISCUS\nPathology specifically involving the medial meniscus.\nlabel=1 for explicit tear, rupture, truncation, extrusion, displaced fragment,\nabnormal morphology, maceration, or other medial meniscus pathology.\nlabel=0 when explicitly normal or preserved.\n\nLATERAL MENISCUS\nSame rules, for the lateral meniscus.\n\nMEDIAL OA\nOsteoarthritis of the MEDIAL tibiofemoral compartment.\nlabel=1 for medial compartment cartilage loss / chondrosis / thinning,\nmarginal osteophytes, subchondral sclerosis, subchondral oedema or cysts in the\nmedial compartment. Indirect wording counts: \"chondrosis\", \"cartilage loss\",\n\"joint space narrowing\" of the medial compartment, \"degenerative change\" of the\nmedial compartment.\nlabel=0 when the medial compartment is explicitly normal or preserved.\n\nLATERAL OA\nSame rules, for the LATERAL tibiofemoral compartment.\n\nPF OA\nOsteoarthritis of the PATellofemoral compartment.\nlabel=1 for patellofemoral cartilage loss / chondrosis, patellar or trochlear\nosteophytes, subchondral change in the patellofemoral compartment.\nlabel=0 when explicitly normal or preserved.\n\nEFFUSION  [v2 - rewritten, this target was badly over-called in v1]\nAbnormally increased intra-articular joint fluid.\nlabel=1 when the report states a joint effusion or increased intra-articular\nfluid of a DEGREE THAT IS BEING REPORTED AS A FINDING.\nImportant calibration notes:\n- Radiologists very often mention a \"small\", \"mild\", \"trace\", \"minimal\" or\n  \"physiological\" amount of fluid as an incidental remark. Judge these on the\n  whole report: if the effusion is small/incidental and not part of the\n  impression or the significant findings, it is usually NOT the effusion this\n  task is asking about -> lean to label 0 with a probability around 0.30-0.45,\n  not 1.\n- A \"moderate\", \"large\", \"marked\", \"massive\" effusion, an effusion that\n  distends the suprapatellar recess or bursa, or one that is listed under\n  IMPRESSION / CONCLUSION, is a genuine positive -> label 1.\n- Fluid in a Baker's cyst, in a paralabral/parameniscal cyst, or in the\n  subcutaneous soft tissues is NOT a joint effusion.\n- If the report only says \"no effusion\", label 0, negated 1.\nGive a GRANULAR probability rather than 0.95/0.05: an equivocal small effusion\nshould sit around 0.4-0.6.\n\nSYNOVITIS  [v2 - rewritten, this target was badly under-called in v1]\nInflammation or abnormality of the synovium. This is the target radiologists\ndescribe INDIRECTLY most often, so read for the following equivalents, not\njust for the word \"synovitis\":\n- explicit synovitis, synovial inflammation, synovitis/aktivierung,\n  sinovitis, 滑膜炎\n- synovial thickening, synovial hypertrophy, synovial proliferation,\n  villonodular / frond-like synovial change\n- HOFFA FAT PAD impingement, Hoffa fat pad oedema / signal change,\n  infrapatellar fat pad oedema, 髌下脂肪垫 (this is a classic indirect\n  description of synovitis/impingement and COUNTS)\n- plica syndrome / medial or lateral plicae when described as thickened,\n  inflamed or symptomatic\n- suprapatellar bursitis when described with synovial hypertrophy or\n  inflammatory change\n- friktionssyndrom / impingement syndrome with an inflammatory component\nlabel=1 when any of the above is present with an inflammatory or proliferative\ncharacter.\nlabel=0 when the synovium is explicitly normal, or when joint fluid alone is\nmentioned without any synovial thickening/ inflammation/ Hoffa oedema.\nNote: a bare joint effusion, on its own, is NOT synovitis.\n\nBAKER'S CYST\nA cyst in the popliteal fossa (gastrocnemius-semimembranosus bursa).\nlabel=1 for an explicit Baker's cyst, popliteal cyst, or Baker-Zyste / quiste\npoplíteo / 腘窝囊肿.\nlabel=0 when explicitly absent or when only a small amount of fluid in a normal\nbursa is seen.\n\nCONTUSION\nBone contusion / bone bruise (trabecular microfracture with marrow oedema).\nlabel=1 for explicit bone contusion, bone bruise, marrow oedema of traumatic\ncharacter, or \"Kontusion\" / \"contusión\" / 骨挫伤.\nDo NOT count isolated subchondral oedema that is clearly attributed to\nosteoarthritis or to stress reaction without trauma wording.\nlabel=0 when explicitly absent.\n\nFRACTURE\nFracture of any bone about the knee.\nlabel=1 for explicit fracture, cortical disruption, avulsion fracture,\noccult fracture, insufficiency fracture, or \"Fraktur\" / \"fractura\" / 骨折.\nlabel=0 when explicitly absent.\n\nOUTPUT\nReturn ONLY a single JSON object, no prose, no markdown fences, with exactly\nthese 12 keys and for each the four fields \"label\" (0 or 1), \"probability\"\n(0.00-1.00), \"mentioned\" (0 or 1), \"negated\" (0 or 1).\n\"\"\"\n\nUSER_TEMPLATE = \"\"\"MRI REPORT:\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\n{report}\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\n\nReturn the JSON object now.\"\"\"\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "## 3. Output parser & normaliser\n\nQwen2.5-32B tends to return SCREAMING_SNAKE keys (`MEDIAL_MENISCUS`, `BAKER'S_CYST`) where the v1 model used title case. The parser pulls the first JSON object, matches keys loosely (also tolerating `Baker's Cyst` / `Bakers`), and enforces label–mentioned–negated logical consistency (`mentioned=0 ⇒ label=0`; `negated=1 ⇒ label=0`).\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "_JSON_RE = re.compile(r\"\\{.*\\}\", re.S)\n\n\ndef parse_json(raw: str) -> dict | None:\n    \"\"\"Pull the first JSON object out of the completion.\"\"\"\n    raw = raw.strip()\n    if raw.startswith(\"```\"):\n        raw = re.sub(r\"^```[a-zA-Z]*\\s*\", \"\", raw)\n        raw = re.sub(r\"\\s*```$\", \"\", raw)\n    for cand in (raw,_JSON_RE.search(raw).group(0) if_JSON_RE.search(raw) else \"\"):\n        try:\n            obj = json.loads(cand)\n            if isinstance(obj, dict):\n                return obj\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n    return None\n\n\n_KEY_NORM = {re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", l.lower()): l for l in LABELS}\n_KEY_ALIAS = {\n    re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", k): v\n    for k, v in [(\"Baker's Cyst\", \"Baker's\"), (\"Baker cyst\", \"Baker's\"),\n                 (\"Bakers\", \"Baker's\"), (\"bakercyst\", \"Baker's\"),\n                 (\"Patellofemoral OA\", \"PF OA\"), (\"PF OA\", \"PF OA\")]\n}\n\n\ndef_match_key(key: str) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Map a model-generated JSON key to a canonical LABELS entry.\n\n    Qwen3-8B (v1) emits \"Medial Meniscus\"; Qwen2.5-32B (v2) normalises keys to\n    \"MEDIAL_MENISCUS\" / \"BAKER'S_CYST\". Match by stripping non-alphanumerics,\n    then fall back to substring matching.\n    \"\"\"\n    nk = re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]\", \"\", (key or \"\").lower())\n    if nk in_KEY_NORM:\n        return_KEY_NORM[nk]\n    if nk in_KEY_ALIAS:\n        return_KEY_ALIAS[nk]\n    for k, lab in_KEY_NORM.items():\n        if k in nk or nk in k:\n            return lab\n    return None\n\n\ndef normalise(obj: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Enforce the output contract, tolerating minor model deviations.\n\n    The v2 prompt returns \"probability\" = the model's calibrated P(present).\n    We keep the CSV column name \"confidence\" for schema compatibility with the\n    deprecated v1 file, but its meaning is now P(present): it is used directly\n    as the ROC score and as the soft label for training.\n    \"\"\"\n    lookup = {}\n    for k, v in (obj or {}).items():\n        m = *match_key(k)\n        if m and isinstance(v, dict):\n            lookup[m] = v\n    res = {}\n    for lab in LABELS:\n        e = lookup.get(lab) or {}\n        lab_v = int(e.get(\"label\", 0))\n        men = int(e.get(\"mentioned\", 0))\n        neg = int(e.get(\"negated\", 0))\n        prob = e.get(\"probability\", e.get(\"confidence\", 0.5))\n        try:\n            prob = float(prob)\n        except Exception:\n            prob = 0.5\n        prob = float(min(max(prob, 0.0), 1.0))\n        # logical consistency (same rules as the v1 generator)\n        if men == 0:\n            neg, lab_v = 0, 0\n        if neg == 1:\n            lab_v = 0\n        res[lab] = {\"label\": lab_v, \"confidence\": prob,\n                    \"mentioned\": men, \"negated\": neg}\n    return res\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "## 4. Load Qwen2.5-32B (4-bit)\n\nLoads the tokenizer and model with automatic dtype/device placement. Requirements for the full run:\n\n* __~20 GB VRAM__ at 4-bit — a 24 GB RTX 4090 / RTX 3090, or Kaggle T4 x2 via `device_map=\"auto\"` sharding.\n* `bitsandbytes`, `accelerate`, `transformers`, `torch`.\n\nThe model is only loaded when `RUN_GENERATION = True` in the configuration cell; everything else in this notebook works without a GPU.\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "def *compute_dtype(torch):\n    \"\"\"Pick the best dtype the GPU actually supports.\n\n    Turing (compute capability 7.5 -- RTX 2080 Ti) has NO bf16 units: asking\n    for bfloat16 either errors out or silently falls back to a very slow\n    simulated path. Ampere (8.0+) and Hopper (9.0) support bf16 natively and\n    bf16 is safer against overflow, so prefer it there.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not torch.cuda.is_available():\n        return torch.float32\n    major, minor = torch.cuda.get_device_capability(0)\n    name = torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)\n    if major >= 8:\n        dt = torch.bfloat16\n        why = f\"sm*{major}{minor} (>=8.0): bf16 supported\"\n    else:\n        dt = torch.float16\n        why = f\"sm*{major}{minor} (<8.0, no bf16 units): forced fp16\"\n    print(f\"[dtype] {name} -> {why}\")\n    return dt\n\n\ndef load_model(model_id: str = MODEL_ID, quant: str = QUANT, dtype: str = \"auto\"):\n    import torch\n    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\n\n    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id, trust_remote_code=True)\n    if tok.pad_token is None:\n        tok.pad_token = tok.eos_token\n\n    if dtype == \"auto\":\n_dt = _compute_dtype(torch)\n    else:\n        _dt = {\"fp16\": torch.float16, \"bf16\": torch.bfloat16,\n               \"fp32\": torch.float32}[dtype]\n\n    kwargs = dict(device_map=\"auto\", trust_remote_code=True)\n    # sdpa works on every GPU we care about and is fine at this batch size.\n    if not getattr(load_model, \"_warned_attn\", False):\n        kwargs[\"attn_implementation\"] = \"sdpa\"\n        load_model._warned_attn = True\n\n    if quant == \"4bit\":\n        from transformers import BitsAndBytesConfig\n        kwargs[\"quantization_config\"] = BitsAndBytesConfig(\n            load_in_4bit=True,\n            bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=_dt,\n            bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,\n            bnb_4bit_quant_type=\"nf4\",\n        )\n    elif quant == \"8bit\":\n        kwargs[\"load_in_8bit\"] = True\n    else:\n        kwargs[\"torch_dtype\"] = _dt\n\n    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(model_id, **kwargs)\n    model.eval()\n    if torch.cuda.is_available():\n        used = torch.cuda.memory_allocated() / 1024** 3\n        total = torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_memory / 1024 **3\n        print(f\"[model] {model_id} loaded  weights={used:.1f}GB / {total:.1f}GB \"\n              f\"free={total - used:.1f}GB\")\n    return tok, model\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "## 5. Inference (batched greedy decoding)\n\nA 32B model is memory-bandwidth bound, so batching is what makes the 4,407-study run feasible: `batch=4` reads the ~20 GB of weights once per four reports instead of once per report (roughly 16 h → 7 h on an RTX 4090). Left padding is used (mandatory for `generate()`), and each output is decoded separately and truncated at its pad token.\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "def extract_one(tok, model, report: str, max_new_tokens: int = 1400) -> str:\n    import torch\n\n    messages = [\n        {\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": SYSTEM_PROMPT},\n        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": USER_TEMPLATE.format(report=report[:6000])},\n    ]\n    text = tok.apply_chat_template(\n        messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True)\n    inputs = tok([text], return_tensors=\"pt\", padding=True).to(model.device)\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        out = model.generate(\n**inputs,\n            max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,\n            do_sample=False,\n            temperature=None,\n            top_p=None,\n            pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id,\n        )\n    gen = out[0][inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]:]\n    return tok.decode(gen, skip_special_tokens=True)\n\n\ndef extract_batch(tok, model, reports, max_new_tokens: int = MAX_NEW_TOKENS,\n                  batch_size: int = BATCH_SIZE):\n    \"\"\"Generate for several reports in ONE forward pass.\n\n    With a 32B model the decode step is memory-bandwidth bound: batch=1 reads\n    the ~20GB weights once per report, batch=4 reads them once per FOUR reports.\n    That is the difference between ~16h and ~7h for the full 4407-study run.\n\n    Left padding is mandatory for generate(): with right padding the model\n    would attend to pad tokens at the END of the sequence. Each output is\n    decoded separately and truncated at the first pad token so one short\n    completion does not get contaminated by the batch tail.\n    \"\"\"\n    import torch\n\n    messages = [\n        [{\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": SYSTEM_PROMPT},\n         {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": USER_TEMPLATE.format(report=r[:6000])}]\n        for r in reports\n    ]\n    texts = [tok.apply_chat_template(m, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True)\n             for m in messages]\n    inputs = tok(texts, return_tensors=\"pt\", padding=True,\n                 padding_side=\"left\").to(model.device)\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        out = model.generate(\n            **inputs,\n            max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,\n            do_sample=False,\n            temperature=None,\n            top_p=None,\n            pad_token_id=tok.pad_token_id,\n        )\n    L = inputs[\"input_ids\"].shape[1]\n    raws = []\n    for g in out[:, L:]:\n        g = g[g != tok.pad_token_id]        # drop any trailing pad tokens\n        raws.append(tok.decode(g, skip_special_tokens=True))\n    return raws\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": [
    "## 6. Run over all studies\n",
    "\n",
    "Flip `RUN_GENERATION = True` in the configuration cell to reproduce the run (`LIMIT` caps the number of studies — set to `None` for all 4,407). It writes:\n",
    "\n",
    "* `weak_labels_v2_raw.csv` — raw model output (checkpointed every 25 rows; re-running starts from scratch)\n",
    "* `weak_labels_v2_filled.csv` — raw output + the co-occurrence priors described in the Model Performance section\n",
    "\n",
    "The official run completed with __4,407 / 4,407 rows `ok` (0 errors)__ in ~7 h on a cloud RTX 4090 (batch=4).\n"
   ]
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Generate the raw labels (optional) and apply the co-occurrence fill\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# Co-occurrence prior rules. Only UNMENTIONED cells may be touched:\n# target mentioned==0 AND partner confidence > 0.5. Fill values are low on\n# purpose (they only nudge the soft label) to avoid overfitting.\n_FILL_RULES = [\n    # (target, partner, fill value)  -- empirical P(target | partner) in parens\n    (\"PF OA\",     \"Medial OA\", 0.60),   # P(PF OA|MedOA) ~ 0.70\n    (\"Synovitis\", \"Medial OA\", 0.60),   # P(Syn |MedOA) ~ 0.74\n    (\"Synovitis\", \"Effusion\",  0.55),   # P(Syn |Eff)   ~ 0.63\n]\n\n\ndef process(raw, uid, report):\n    \"\"\"Parse one completion into the output record. Raises on failure.\"\"\"\n    ts = time.time()\n    obj = parse_json(raw)\n    if obj is None:\n        raise ValueError(\"no JSON object in completion\")\n    res = normalise(obj)\n    rec = {\"StudyInstanceUID\": uid, \"Report\": report,\n           \"qwen_raw\": raw[:4000], \"status\": \"ok\", \"error\": \"\",\n           \"generation_seconds\": round(time.time() - ts, 2)}\n    for lab in LABELS:\n        r = res[lab]\n        rec[f\"{lab}__label\"] = r[\"label\"]\n        rec[f\"{lab}__confidence\"] = r[\"confidence\"]\n        rec[f\"{lab}__mentioned\"] = r[\"mentioned\"]\n        rec[f\"{lab}__negated\"] = r[\"negated\"]\n    return rec\n\n\ndef_prepare_rows(limit):\n    if not os.path.exists(TRAIN_CSV):\n        raise FileNotFoundError(TRAIN_CSV)\n    tr = pd.read_csv(TRAIN_CSV, low_memory=False)\n    tr[\"StudyInstanceUID\"] = tr[\"StudyInstanceUID\"].astype(str)\n    tr = tr.dropna(subset=[\"Report\"])\n    tr[\"Report\"] = tr[\"Report\"].astype(str)\n    tr = tr[~tr[\"StudyInstanceUID\"].duplicated()]\n    if limit:\n        # prefer gold-labelled studies so a small run still scores against the\n        # 58 expert studies (train.csv has exactly 58 non-null rows)\n        if \"ACL\" in tr.columns:\n            gold_mask = tr[\"ACL\"].notna()\n            g, ng = tr[gold_mask], tr[~gold_mask]\n            if len(g) >= limit:\n                tr = g.head(limit)\n            else:\n                tr = pd.concat([g, ng.head(limit - len(g))])\n        else:\n            tr = tr.head(limit)\n    return tr.reset_index(drop=True)\n\n\ndef run_generation(limit=LIMIT):\n    \"\"\"Full/reproduce run. Enabled by setting RUN_GENERATION = True above.\"\"\"\n    if not RUN_GENERATION:\n        print(\"RUN_GENERATION = False -> generation skipped. Flip it to True \"\n              \"in the config cell to reproduce the labels from train.csv.\")\n        return\n    if not os.path.exists(TRAIN_CSV):\n        print(f\"train.csv not found at {TRAIN_CSV} - nothing to generate.\")\n        return\n    print(f\"[run] model={MODEL_ID} quant={QUANT} limit={limit} \"\n          f\"batch_size={BATCH_SIZE}\")\n    tok, model = load_model()\n\n    rows = [dict(r) for _, r in_prepare_rows(limit).iterrows()]\n    # length-sorted order so left-padding wastes almost nothing\n    order = sorted(range(len(rows)),\n                   key=lambda i: len(str(rows[i].get(\"Report\", \"\") or \"\")))\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    done = 0\n    rows_by_uid = {}\n    for s in range(0, len(order), BATCH_SIZE):\n        idxs = order[s:s + BATCH_SIZE]\n        reports = [str(rows[i].get(\"Report\", \"\") or \"\") for i in idxs]\n        try:\n            raws = extract_batch(tok, model, reports)\n        except Exception as e:\n            # whole batch failed (e.g. OOM): fall back to one-by-one so a\n            # single bad report cannot kill the run\n            print(f\"  batch of {len(idxs)} failed ({type(e).__name__}: {e}); \"\n                  f\"retrying individually\", flush=True)\n            raws = []\n            for rpt in reports:\n                try:\n                    raws.append(extract_one(tok, model, rpt))\n                except Exception as e2:\n                    raws.append(f\"__ERROR__ {type(e2).__name__}: {e2}\")\n        for i, raw in zip(idxs, raws):\n            uid = str(rows[i][\"StudyInstanceUID\"])\n            report = str(rows[i].get(\"Report\", \"\") or \"\")\n            try:\n                rec = process(raw, uid, report)\n            except Exception as e:  # keep the pipeline alive on one bad report\n                rec = {\"StudyInstanceUID\": uid, \"Report\": report,\n                       \"qwen_raw\": raw[:4000], \"status\": \"error\",\n                       \"error\": f\"{type(e).__name__}: {e}\"[:300],\n                       \"generation_seconds\": 0.0}\n                for lab in LABELS:\n                    rec[f\"{lab}__label\"] = 0\n                    rec[f\"{lab}__confidence\"] = 0.5\n                    rec[f\"{lab}__mentioned\"] = 0\n                    rec[f\"{lab}__negated\"] = 0\n            rows_by_uid[uid] = rec\n        done += len(idxs)\n        if done % 25 < BATCH_SIZE or done == len(order):\n            el = time.time() - t0\n            print(f\"  {done}/{len(order)}  {el/60:.1f} min elapsed  \"\n                  f\"({el/done:.2f} s/report)\", flush=True)\n            pd.DataFrame(list(rows_by_uid.values())).to_csv(\n                RAW_OUT, index=False)   # checkpoint\n        if len(rows_by_uid) >= (limit or len(rows)):\n            break\n\n    pd.DataFrame(list(rows_by_uid.values())).to_csv(RAW_OUT, index=False)\n    print(f\"[done] wrote {RAW_OUT}  ({len(rows_by_uid)} rows, \"\n          f\"{(time.time()-t0)/60:.1f} min)\")\n\n\ndef apply_fill(d, verbose=True):\n    \"\"\"Conservative co-occurrence priors on UNMENTIONED cells only.\n\n    Only the ``confidence`` column is modified; label/mentioned/negated are\n    left untouched. ``confidence = max(confidence, fill)`` so this can only\n    raise the soft label of a silent-but-likely finding.\n    \"\"\"\n    d = d.copy()\n    for target, partner, pval in _FILL_RULES:\n        mn = pd.to_numeric(d[f\"{target}__mentioned\"], errors=\"coerce\") \\\n            .fillna(0).to_numpy()\n        pp = pd.to_numeric(d[f\"{partner}__confidence\"], errors=\"coerce\") \\\n            .fillna(0).to_numpy()\n        c = np.array(pd.to_numeric(d[f\"{target}__confidence\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n                     .to_numpy(), dtype=np.float64)\n        mask = (mn == 0) & (pp > 0.5)\n        c[mask] = np.maximum(c[mask], pval)\n        d[f\"{target}__confidence\"] = c\n        if verbose:\n            print(f\"  fill {target:<10} <- {partner:<10} ({pval:.2f}): \"\n                  f\"{int(mask.sum())} cells\")\n    return d\n\n\n# Generate raw labels first, then derive the filled production file.\nrun_generation()\n\nif os.path.exists(RAW_OUT):\n    raw_df = pd.read_csv(RAW_OUT, low_memory=False)\n    filled_df = apply_fill(raw_df)\n    filled_df.to_csv(FILL_OUT, index=False)\n    print(f\"wrote {FILL_OUT}  ({len(filled_df)} rows)\")\nelse:\n    print(f\"(no {RAW_OUT} - run_generation() was skipped)\")\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "## 7. Validation vs the 58 gold studies (macro ROC AUC)\n\nThe competition metric is __macro ROC AUC__ = mean of the 12 per-target ROC AUCs. Pooled ROC AUC (one curve over all 696 decisions) is __not__ the metric and is printed below for reference only, clearly labelled.\n\nThis cell reads the two official files from the output dataset (no GPU needed) and re-derives the published numbers; freshly generated files are validated automatically too when `RUN_GENERATION = True`.\n"
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": "# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Validation against the 58 gold studies (macro ROC AUC = competition metric)\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef validate(out_csv, train_csv, title=\"VALIDATION\"):\n    from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score\n\n    pred = pd.read_csv(out_csv, low_memory=False)\n    tr = pd.read_csv(train_csv, low_memory=False)\n    for d in (pred, tr):\n        d[\"StudyInstanceUID\"] = d[\"StudyInstanceUID\"].astype(str)\n\n    gold = tr[tr[\"ACL\"].notna()]\n    df = gold[[\"StudyInstanceUID\"] + LABELS].merge(\n        pred.drop(columns=[c for c in (\"Report\",) if c in pred.columns]),\n        on=\"StudyInstanceUID\", how=\"inner\")\n    if len(df) == 0:\n        print(f\"[validate] no overlap with the gold studies - skip\")\n        return\n\n    per, yt, ys = [], [], []\n    for lab in LABELS:\n        g = df[lab].astype(int)\n        col = f\"{lab}__label\"\n        if col not in df.columns:\n            continue\n        p = pd.to_numeric(df[col], errors=\"coerce\").fillna(0).astype(int)\n        # v2 semantics: confidence == calibrated P(present), use directly as\n        # the score. (v1 confidence was extraction certainty - not comparable.)\n        score = pd.to_numeric(\n            df[f\"{lab}__confidence\"], errors=\"coerce\").fillna(0.5).to_numpy()\n        if len(np.unique(g)) < 2:\n            continue\n        a = roc_auc_score(g, score)\n        per.append((lab, a, float(g.mean()), float(p.mean())))\n        yt.extend(g.tolist())\n        ys.extend(score.tolist())\n\n    macro = float(np.mean([a for_, a, _,_ in per]))\n    pooled = roc_auc_score(yt, ys) if len(set(yt)) > 1 else float(\"nan\")\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" *62)\n    print(title)\n    print(\"=\"* 62)\n    print(f\"{'target':<18}{'AUC':>9}{'gold_prev':>11}{'pred_prev':>11}\")\n    for lab, a, gp, pp in sorted(per, key=lambda r: r[1]):\n        print(f\"{lab:<18}{a:>9.4f}{gp:>11.2f}{pp:>11.2f}\")\n    print(\"-\" *62)\n    print(f\"{'MACRO (competition metric)':<18}{macro:>9.4f}\")\n    print(f\"{'POOLED (not the metric)':<18}{pooled:>9.4f}\")\n    print(\"=\"* 62)\n\n\nprint(\"Official outputs shipped in the dataset:\")\nfor path, tag in [(OFFICIAL_RAW, \"raw labels   \"),\n                  (OFFICIAL_FILL, \"filled labels\")]:\n    if os.path.exists(path):\n        validate(path, TRAIN_CSV, title=f\"VALIDATION - {tag.strip()} \"\n                                        f\"(58 gold studies)\")\n    else:\n        print(f\"  skip {tag}: {path} not mounted\")\n\nif RUN_GENERATION and os.path.exists(RAW_OUT):\n    validate(RAW_OUT, TRAIN_CSV, title=\"VALIDATION - freshly generated RAW\")\n    if os.path.exists(FILL_OUT):\n        validate(FILL_OUT, TRAIN_CSV, title=\"VALIDATION - freshly generated FILLED\")\n"
  }

RSNA Knee Abnormality Detection
Create a model that can detect knee abnormalities based on multimodal imaging data

YYama · 14th in this Competition · Posted 16 days ago
Please clarify which external knee MRI datasets are allowed
Thank you to the hosts for organizing this challenge and for taking the time to answer questions throughout the competition. I appreciate that assessing third-party datasets individually is difficult, especially when their access requirements and licenses differ.

Questions about external knee MRI data are spread across several threads. Without a clear answer for commonly used datasets, teams may train under different assumptions about what is permitted, which makes it difficult to ensure a level playing field.

I have read the original question, the registration-gated dataset question, and the institutional-agreement follow-up. There are also separate questions about pretrained weights and research-use agreements.

The host has explicitly ruled that KneeCoT is not allowed because its formal institutional agreement may create unequal access. The host has also explained that non-commercial terms alone do not exclude a dataset, while straightforward registration and substantial institutional approval may be treated differently. It remains unclear to me how these principles apply to the following resources:

Resource Relevant access or licensing issue
Stanford MRNet Research-use agreement
Stanford SKM-TEA Registration and data-use terms
NYU fastMRI and fastMRI+ Data-sharing agreement for images; separately released annotations
Osteoarthritis Initiative (OAI) Data Use Certification and institutional requirements
KneeMRI CC BY-NC-ND 4.0
KneeXNet-2.5D Public OAI-derived annotated images and pretrained weights; no explicit repository license that I could identify
Could the hosts give a yes or no for each resource under this competition's external-data rules? If the answer differs between training on the images and fine-tuning publicly released weights, please state that separately. It would also help to know whether using each resource is compatible with the winner's requirement to release the final model. I understand that teams remain responsible for complying with the original providers' terms.

Because the same question keeps appearing in different threads, could the hosts pin the answer in the Discussion so all teams can find and follow the same guidance?

@javaduke95

· 14th in this Competition

I would like to follow up on this question, as the status of several commonly used knee MRI datasets is still unclear.

Resource Host status Access / licensing issue
KneeCoT Prohibited (host ruling) Download is technically easy, but the dataset card requires a formal ethical-use agreement with the affiliated hospital
Stanford MRNet Unclear Stanford AIMI / Redivis access; subject to research-use terms and dataset-specific access requirements
Stanford SKM-TEA Unclear Stanford AIMI / Redivis access; registration and dataset-specific access requirements
NYU fastMRI / fastMRI+ Unclear Application required, including institutional affiliation and proposed use; Data Sharing Agreement
OAI Unclear NDA registration / controlled-access process; not simply an unrestricted public download
KneeMRI Unclear Public download, but CC BY-NC-ND 4.0; unclear whether training/releasing model weights is compatible with the NoDerivatives clause
KneeXNet-2.5D Unclear Publicly released OAI-derived annotations/models; repository says research/educational use, but no explicit repository license identified
Given that KneeCoT is prohibited because of its institutional agreement requirement, could the hosts clarify whether datasets requiring comparable or more substantial access procedures are also prohibited?

At minimum, could the KneeCoT prohibition be pinned? Although the dataset card explicitly requires a formal ethical-use agreement with the affiliated hospital, the files themselves are relatively easy to access, and this requirement may easily be overlooked in practice. Given the dataset’s large scale and its inclusion of knee MRI examinations, diagnostic reports, labels, VQA data, and reasoning annotations that closely overlap with this competition’s tasks, its use could potentially lead to substantial score gains. A pinned notice would help prevent inadvertent use and ensure that all teams are operating under the same rules.

NguyenThanhNhan
Posted 4 days ago

· 30th in this Competition

I find the calls for a strict external-data ban in the name of “fairness” pretty LAUGHABLE 😀. How exactly is this supposed to be enforced, and what level playing field are we pretending exists?

We can use LLMs from Google, OpenAI, or Anthropic to extract labels from radiology reports or help annotate knee MRIs. We don’t know their complete pretraining and post-training datasets. I’m not claiming they were trained on any particular knee MRI dataset, but if these teachers are allowed, how do we establish that their labels contain no benefit from external data? Where exactly is the boundary?

In my experience, datasets such as fastMRI, fastMRI+, OAI, and MRNet are readily accessible because:

You don't need an academic institution email
You don't have to sign any agreement and wait for human approval
How would organizers verify that a winning model never used them? Rerunning an inference notebook verifies its predictions, not its training history. Independently reproducing the full training pipeline requires substantial time and compute that most participants cannot spare.

And if the argument is equal opportunity, what about participants working with two T4 GPUs and Kaggle’s limited weekly quota while others train on clusters of H200s/ B200s/ B300s ? An external-data ban leaves that disparity intact. If equal resources are the principle, apply it to compute too.

Reply

React
This comment has been deleted.

RSNA Knee Abnormality Detection
Create a model that can detect knee abnormalities based on multimodal imaging data

colum2131 · 52nd in this Competition · Posted 2 days ago
Local CV or Public LB: which one can we trust?
In this competition, the only expert-labelled data available for local validation are the 58 gold training studies. That makes local validation unusually challenging.

I compared two local validation scores against the public LB across 15 of my own submissions, each based on five-fold CV:

AUC on the 58 gold studies vs. public LB: r = 0.66.
AUC on ~4,300 studies with LLM-extracted labels vs. public LB: r = 0.87.
For my submissions, higher CV scores on the LLM-extracted (weak) labels have generally gone hand in hand with higher public LB scores. AUC on the 58 gold studies tracked the public LB less closely.

My current thinking:

The public LB uses ~30% of the test set, which is much larger than 58 studies. It may therefore provide a more reliable signal, though sample size alone doesn't guarantee that.
For local validation, evaluating out-of-fold predictions against the LLM-extracted labels across the training set might be the most practical option after all.
I'd love to hear how others are approaching validation here, and whether you've seen similar patterns. I'm also curious how much of a leaderboard shake-up people expect at the end.

Slightly off-topic, but since the metric is AUC: submitting 1 − p instead of p gives you 1 − AUC on the public LB. Because the leaderboard only displays each team's best score, a team could use this to track improvements without revealing its actual performance, then flip the predictions back at the end.

So there may be some strong teams flying under the radar. It'll be interesting to watch the LB in the final days.

(yu4u and tattaka did very well in past RSNA competitions, yet their team is sitting at just 0.949 on the public LB right now, which feels a little suspicious 👀)

React

Post Comment
Mingquan Zhuang
Posted 5 hours ago

· 2985th in this Competition

The 0.87 vs 0.66 comparison is useful, but with 15 related submissions I would treat the ranking as provisional. The submissions share folds, training data and likely model components, so those 15 points are not independent. A practical check would be to compare paired changes between adjacent model versions: for each change, did weak-label OOF AUC, gold-58 AUC, and public LB move in the same direction? Report this per target label as well as for the aggregate. For the weak labels, keep all predictions strictly out of fold at study/patient level and stratify by label-extraction confidence if available. I would use the public LB as a secondary check, since choosing repeatedly against it can overfit that split even when its sample is larger. This would help distinguish a stable validation signal from a correlation driven by a few related experiments.

Reply

hengck23
Topic Author
Posted 10 hours ago

· 1414th in this Competition

I haven't tried Ortho Foundation model yet, but maybe someone tried it before me. I think I have a solution to 58-shots training.

I have tried many many many many experiments. The onlymethod that works without overfitting and shakeup is:

1) use 58 samples to train a model. if this models uses only very little parameters (e.g. only 10), it is unlikely to overfit. there is a mathematical or probabilistic relationship between parameter size, sample size and overfitting.
2) you can use an open-source pretrained model with mostly frozen weights. You can use SSL and finetune, etc … 3) basically when there is not enough sample supervision, prior is the key

Reply

hengck23
Topic Author
Posted 2 days ago

· 1414th in this Competition

How to get good training labels? One way to look at this Kaggle competition is to create soft/hard study-level labels to train an image MRI model.

1) we start with Claude Opus 4.5 labels : <https://github.com/NTejas-1/RSNA-Knee-Abnormality-Detection>
2) we use non-gold 4.3k labels to train mri and text models
3) we blend them : local cv on gold58 = 0.938

hengck23
Topic Author
Posted 7 days ago

· 1414th in this Competition

The same old tricks in the old days of kaggle:

1) for "solo devlopement" : active labelling. Starts ask chatgot to label lots of slices. Then train some models (or grab from public notebooks) and ask chatgpt to verify only smaller number of suspicious slices. Since the views (and image mode) are related, we just need to label one view, etc

2) for"team effort" (which i preferred). Once someone has a good prompt and an efficient and effective workflow/harness, kagglers can share the token cost of compute once that is open.

3) of course there is local VLA deployment if you have a good cloud or server/desktop GPU.

i just downloaded the images, i am trying to make baseline and then labeling. i will report results if i work fast enough

%%writefile /tmp/raptor_run.py

# Authorized scoring run: verify the selected checkpoint before inference

RUN_INFERENCE = True
if not RUN_INFERENCE:
    raise SystemExit("Static notebook only. Attach the exact checkpoint and competition, enable a T4 GPU, and set RUN_INFERENCE = True to run inference.")

import hashlib
from pathlib import Path
import torch
if not torch.cuda.is_available():
    raise RuntimeError("This scored pipeline requires CUDA; CPU inference is not enabled.")
_weight_name = 'raptor_ft_alldata_t16_blendjev_oai_d96_r384_swa.pt'
_candidates = [
    Path('/kaggle/input/rsna-knee-publication-swa-weights-20261007') / _weight_name,
    Path('/kaggle/input/datasets/nartaa/rsna-knee-publication-swa-weights-20261007') / _weight_name,
    Path('/kaggle/input/rsna-knee-oai-original384-s42-pair') / _weight_name,
    Path('/kaggle/input/datasets/nartaa/rsna-knee-oai-original384-s42-pair') / _weight_name,
]
_weights = next((p for p in_candidates if p.is_file()), None)
if_weights is None:
    raise FileNotFoundError("Attach the exact selected weights; no alternative model is substituted.")
with _weights.open('rb') as_f:
    assert hashlib.file_digest(_f, 'sha256').hexdigest() == '7e5315dad125b99fc65b340b3de41de628e9be51ff5835355dd61c86472244ef', "Checkpoint hash differs"

# %% [code]

# !/usr/bin/env python3
"""Knee MRI: twelve findings from a single model

This notebook takes a knee MRI study and scores twelve findings at once: ACL tear, MCL tear,
medial and lateral meniscus tears, osteoarthritis in the medial, lateral and patellofemoral
compartments, joint effusion, synovitis, a Baker's cyst, bone contusion and fracture. It scores
0.924 on the public leaderboard using one model, with no ensembling and no test-time augmentation.

This is the inference half of the work. The model was trained separately and its weights are
attached as a dataset, so this notebook only loads them and predicts:
<https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/dreaddevelopment/raptor-knee-widedense>

Where the training labels came from

Worth saying up front, because it shapes everything else. The competition gives you 4,407 studies
but structured labels for only 58 of them. Every other study arrives with a free-text radiology
report and nothing more, so there is very little to train against out of the box.

The labels behind these weights were made by reading those reports with a language model and
turning each into twelve probabilities rather than twelve yes or no answers. A report that says a
tear is suspected becomes a number near 0.8, not a 1, which is a fairer target than forcing every
hedged sentence into a hard label. That yields 4,349 studies to train on. The 58 studies that came
with real labels were never trained on and are used to check the result honestly; the model reaches
0.9167 macro-AUC on them.

Building a fixed input from studies that are all shaped differently

The hard part of this competition is not the network, it is that no two studies look alike. A
study holds several DICOM series shot in different planes, the number of series varies, and the
number of slices in a series varies more. Anything that expects a fixed-size input has to be given
one.

The approach here is to fill five fixed slots per study, always in the same order, for a stack of
64 images:

  18 slices from a sagittal series, preferring a fluid-sensitive one
  14 slices from a second sagittal series, preferring one that is not fluid-sensitive
  12 slices from a coronal series, preferring a fluid-sensitive one
   8 slices from a second coronal series
  12 slices from an axial series

Preferring a fluid-sensitive series for some slots and not for others is deliberate. Fluid-
sensitive sequences show swelling, effusion and acute injury clearly, while the other sequences
show anatomy and cartilage better, and the twelve findings are split across both. If a study has
no series for a slot, the slot is left as zeros and the model is told to skip it rather than being
fed something misleading.

Within a series, slices are taken evenly across 6 to 94 percent of the stack rather than from the
middle. The outer slices are where the collateral ligaments and the lateral meniscus sit, and
cutting them was measurably costing accuracy on exactly those findings.

Every slice is cropped to a 140 mm box around the centre of the image using the pixel spacing from
the DICOM header, then resized to 336 pixels. Cropping by millimetres rather than by pixel count
matters: it means a knee occupies the same fraction of the frame whether the scan was acquired at
0.3 or 0.5 mm per pixel, so the model is not asked to learn scale differences that carry no medical
information.

How the model reads the stack

Three neighbouring slices are stacked into the three channels of one image. The network then sees
a little of what lies above and below the slice in the middle, which is most of the benefit of a 3D
model at the cost of a 2D one. Each of these three-slice windows is passed through a CoAtNet
backbone at 384 pixels.

The windows are combined with an attention layer that has separate weights for each of the twelve
findings. This is the part that matters most. A cruciate tear may be visible on two sagittal slices
while osteoarthritis is spread across many coronal ones, and a single pooled score forces those two
to share one notion of which slices are important. Giving each finding its own attention weights
lets each one draw on the slices that actually show it.

Running it

Scoring uses 42 windows per study. Inference runs in half precision and automatically retries a
study in full precision if it fails, so no study is ever dropped from the submission. The notebook
needs no internet: the backbone is loaded from the attached weights rather than downloaded.
"""
import os, sys, glob, time, json, gc
os.environ.setdefault("HF_HUB_OFFLINE", "1")
os.environ.setdefault("TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE", "1")
os.environ.setdefault("HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY", "1")
import numpy as np
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
import threading, queue
import torch, torch.nn as nn, torch.nn.functional as F
import timm

# T4 (Turing) cuDNN v9 has fp16/fp32 conv engines but NOT bf16 for these shapes

# ("GET was unable to find an engine..."); benchmark lets it pick a valid algo for

# the fixed (1,24,3,res,res) input

torch.backends.cudnn.benchmark = True
torch.backends.cuda.matmul.allow_tf32 = True

# ---- fixed config (must match training exactly) -----------------------------

INPUT_RES = 320
INPUT_MODE = "crop"
IMG = 384   # native 384 corpus: the model saw 384 px crops directly, no 336->384 upsample
CROP_MM = 140.0

# 96 slices per study at native 384 px. Must match the corpus the weights were trained on

# (cache/build_corpus96.py)

SLOTS = [("Sagittal", 1, 26), ("Sagittal", 0, 22), ("Coronal", 1, 18),
         ("Coronal", 0, 12), ("Axial", -1, 18)]
MAXS = sum(s[2] for s in SLOTS)                     # 96
K_EVAL = 94   # every window position of the 96-slice test volume (full coverage)
HDR_THREADS = 16   # per prep process: header reads are fuse-latency-bound, not CPU-bound
PX_THREADS = 8     # per prep process: pixel reads of the ~96 picked slices
QUEUE_MAX = 48     # studies in flight between the prep pool and the GPU consumers (RAM bound: ~14 MB each)
NORM = "imagenet"
FASTREAD_VARIANT = 'study_lut'   # kernel v3: research/fastread/readers.py, byte-identical to build_study
FR_HDR, FR_PX = 32, 16
_FASTREAD_B64 = def_load_fastread():
    import base64 as_b, importlib.util as _iu
    p = '/kaggle/working/_fastread.py' if os.path.isdir('/kaggle/working') else os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), '_fastread.py')
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        open(p, 'wb').write(_b.b64decode(_FASTREAD_B64))
    spec = _iu.spec_from_file_location('_fastread', p); m =_iu.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(m)
    return m
LAB = ["ACL", "MCL", "Medial Meniscus", "Lateral Meniscus", "Medial OA", "Lateral OA",
       "PF OA", "Effusion", "Synovitis", "Baker's", "Contusion", "Fracture"]
_MEAN = torch.tensor([0.485, 0.456, 0.406]).view(3, 1, 1)
_STD = torch.tensor([0.229, 0.224, 0.225]).view(3, 1, 1)

# Three arms: (weights filename, fallback arch, fallback res). ck carries arch+res too

# Selected 2026-08-19 by greedy forward selection AND exhaustive subset search over a 7-arm

# panel on the 45-study gold set (phase2/blend_panel.py); both agree on this exact set

# Singles: coatnet384 0.9025 | swinbase384 0.8825 | effv2l480 0.8716

# Blend {coatnet+swin+effv2l} = 0.9068 (2-arm {coatnet+swin} = 0.9059, coatnet alone 0.9025)

# Dropped as redundant: cnn336 (0.8833, the former champion), cnbase384 (0.8754)

# cnlarge384 (0.8752), maxvit384 (0.8438)

#

# SINGLE ARM: coatnet_rmlp_2_rw_384 retrained on the EXPANDED 4,349-study corpus

#

# Why one arm and not the 3-arm blend: on the live leaderboard CoAtNet alone scored 0.914 while

# every blend scored 0.914-0.915, so ensembling is worth ~+0.001 there -- the ~+0.010 it showed

# on the old 45-study gold set was gold-set noise. One arm is also 1/3 the kernel runtime

#

# Corpus expansion: the corpus previously held 3,200 of the 4,349 labelled studies and only 45

# of the 58 gold studies. Rebuilt to 4,407 studies (+37.8% training data, 58-study gate)

#

# Measured on the 58-study gate (the incumbent re-scored on the SAME gate for a fair compare)

# incumbent CoAtNet (3,155-study corpus) 0.8923

# this model       (4,349-study corpus) 0.9054   (+0.0131, better in 92.7% of 2000 bootstraps)

# Biggest gains land on the findings that were capping us: Lateral Meniscus +0.071

# Fracture +0.057, Lateral OA +0.048, Medial Meniscus +0.035, ACL +0.028

ARMS = [   # our 5-fold CoAtNet (public recipe reproduced on our folds/labels), equal-weight rank-mean
    {"file": "raptor_ft_alldata_t16_blendjev_oai_d96_r384_swa.pt", "arch": "coatnet_rmlp_2_rw_384.sw_in12k_ft_in1k", "res": 384, "w": 1.0},

# ============================================================================

# Model -- verbatim from finetune_raptor.py

# ============================================================================

def build_backbone(arch, pretrained=False):
    # maxvit/maxxvit/coatnet are conv-attention hybrids: NO CLS token, NO interpolatable
    # pos-embed -> avg pool. The "vit" substring in "coatnet"/"maxvit" must NOT route them
    # down the ViT path (mirrors finetune_raptor.py exactly).
    hybrid = arch.startswith(("maxvit", "maxxvit", "coatnet", "coat_", "convnext"))
    is_vit = (not hybrid) and any(k in arch for k in ("vit", "deit", "dinov2", "eva", "beit"))
    kw = dict(pretrained=pretrained, num_classes=0, in_chans=3, img_size=INPUT_RES)
    if is_vit:
        kw.update(global_pool="token", dynamic_img_size=True)
    else:
        kw.update(global_pool="avg")
    return timm.create_model(arch, **kw)

class RaptorClassifier(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, backbone, F_dim=768, n=12, drop=0.2):
        super().__init__()
        self.backbone = backbone
        self.norm = nn.LayerNorm(F_dim)
        self.att = nn.Sequential(nn.Linear(F_dim, 256), nn.Tanh(), nn.Dropout(drop),
                                 nn.Linear(256, n))
        self.clsW = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(n, F_dim))
        self.clsb = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(n))
        nn.init.trunc_normal_(self.clsW, std=0.02)
        self.n = n

    def encode(self, x):
        B, K = x.shape[:2]
        # Bound backbone memory; the attention head still sees every window together.
        flat = x.flatten(0, 1)
        f = torch.cat([self.backbone(chunk) for chunk in flat.split(16)], dim=0)
        return f.view(B, K, -1)

    def head(self, feats):
        h = self.norm(feats)
        a = self.att(h)
        a = torch.softmax(a, dim=1)
        pooled = torch.einsum("bkn,bkf->bnf", a, h)
        logits = (pooled * self.clsW).sum(-1) + self.clsb
        return logits

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.head(self.encode(x))

def load_model(pt_path, arch_default, res_default, device, ngpu=1):
    import hashlib
    with open(pt_path, "rb") as stream:
        observed_sha = hashlib.file_digest(stream, "sha256").hexdigest()
    assert observed_sha == '7e5315dad125b99fc65b340b3de41de628e9be51ff5835355dd61c86472244ef', "Bound checkpoint bytes differ"
    print(f"[weights] sha256 {observed_sha}", flush=True)
    ck = torch.load(pt_path, map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
    arch = ck.get("arch", arch_default)
    assert int(ck.get("res", res_default)) == 384, "Expected original 384 checkpoint"
    ck_res = INPUT_RES
    bb = build_backbone(arch, pretrained=False)
    model = RaptorClassifier(bb, F_dim=bb.num_features)
    model.load_state_dict(ck["model"], strict=True)
    model.eval().to(device)
    # NOTE: DataParallel removed on purpose. On the full hidden test it drove a system-RAM OOM
    # (per-forward module replication over many studies); a single T4 handles K_EVAL=24 windows
    # fine. Arms are also run SEQUENTIALLY (see main) so peak RAM == one model, not two.
    del ck
    gc.collect()
    return model, ck_res

# ============================================================================

# Eval windowing -- verbatim from finetune_raptor.py StudyWindows (train=False)

# ============================================================================

def _eval_centers(mask, D, k):
    valid = np.where[mask > 0](0)
    if len(valid) < 3:
        valid = np.arange(min(3, D))
    lo, hi = int(valid.min()), int(valid.max())
    cs = [c for c in range(lo + 1, hi) if c - 1 >= lo and c + 1 <= hi]
    if not cs:
        cs = [max(1, min((lo + hi) // 2, D - 2))]
    idx = np.linspace(0, len(cs) - 1, k).round().astype(int)
    return [cs[i] for i in idx]

SLOT_SIZES = [26, 22, 18, 12, 18]
SAGITTAL_SLOTS = (0, 1)

def *slot_index(D):
    b = np.cumsum([0] + SLOT_SIZES)
    if b[-1] != D: raise RuntimeError(f'slot sizes sum to {b[-1]}, volume has {D} slices')
    s* = np.searchsorted(b, np.arange(D), side='right') - 1
    rel = (np.arange(D) - b[s_]) / np.maximum(1, np.array[SLOT_SIZES](s_) - 1)
    return s_, rel

def *mirror_tri(tri, cs, D):
    """final-g1-bundle MIRROR_TTA: true L/R mirror of (k,3,H,W) uint8 eval triplets (research/rnd4/mirror/mirror_f0.py). The slot of each
    window's CENTRE slice decides the axis: sagittal (slots 0,1) -> reverse the 3 slice-channels; coronal/axial (2,3,4) -> flip the width.
    Done on uint8 BEFORE the per-channel LUT, i.e. 'mirror the knee, then normalise'."""
    s*, _= *slot_index(D)
    sag = torch.from_numpy(np.isin(s*[np.asarray(cs, dtype=np.int64)], SAGITTAL_SLOTS)).to(tri.device).view(-1, 1, 1, 1)
    return torch.where(sag, tri.flip(1), tri.flip(-1))

def infer_pair_probs(model, plain, mirrored, device):
    assert plain.shape==mirrored.shape==(94,3,320,320)
    assert all(not m.training for m in model.modules()), "TTA must not update BN or enable dropout"
    p = infer_probs(model, plain, device)
    q = infer_probs(model, mirrored, device)
    return 0.5 * (p + q)

def eval_windows(vol, mask, k, res, norm=NORM, mirror=False):
    D = vol.shape[0]
    cs =_eval_centers(mask, D, k)
    wins = np.empty((len(cs), 3, res, res), np.float32)
    for j, c in enumerate(cs):
        c = max(1, min(c, D - 2))
        tri = np.stack([vol[c - 1], vol[c], vol[c + 1]], 0)
        if mirror:
            tri =_mirror_tri(torch.from_numpy[tri](None), [c], D)[0].numpy()
        tri = tri.astype(np.float32) / 255.0
        assert tri.shape[-2:] == (IMG, IMG) and res == INPUT_RES
        lo = (IMG - res) // 2
        tri = tri[:, lo:lo + res, lo:lo + res]
        t = torch.from_numpy(np.ascontiguousarray(tri))
        if t.shape[-1] != res:
            t = F.interpolate(t[None], size=(res, res), mode="bilinear",
                              align_corners=False)[0]
        wins[j] = t.numpy()
    x = torch.from_numpy(wins)
    if norm == "imagenet":
        x = (x - _MEAN) /_STD
    return x

_WLUT = {}
def gpu_windows(vol, mask, k, res, dev, norm=NORM, mirror=False):
    """Same windows as eval_windows, built on the GPU from the uint8 volume. Values come from a per-channel
    256-entry table computed on the CPU with eval_windows' own float32 ops, so they are bit-identical."""
    D = vol.shape[0]
    cs = np.array([max(1, min(c, D - 2)) for c in _eval_centers(mask, D, k)])
    tri = torch.from_numpy(np.ascontiguousarray(vol[np.stack([cs - 1, cs, cs + 1], 1)])).to(dev)   # (k,3,IMG,IMG) uint8
    if mirror:
        tri =_mirror_tri(tri, cs, D)
    assert tri.shape[-2:] == (IMG, IMG) and res == INPUT_RES
    lo = (IMG - res) // 2
    tri = tri[:, :, lo:lo + res, lo:lo + res]
    key = (str(dev), norm)
    if key not in _WLUT:
        v = torch.from_numpy((np.arange(256, dtype=np.uint8).astype(np.float32) / 255.0))
        t = torch.stack([v, v, v], 0).view(3, 256, 1)
        if norm == 'imagenet':
            t = (t - _MEAN.view(3, 1, 1)) /_STD.view(3, 1, 1)
        _WLUT[key] = t.view(3, 256).contiguous().to(dev)
    L =_WLUT[key]
    x = torch.stack([L[c][tri[:, c].long()] for c in range(3)], 1)                   # exact float32 values
    if x.shape[-1] != res:
        if norm == 'imagenet':   # resize must happen BEFORE normalisation, as in eval_windows
            L0 =_WLUT.setdefault((str(dev), 'none'), torch.from_numpy(np.arange(256, dtype=np.uint8).astype(np.float32) / 255.0).to(dev))
            x = L0[tri.long()]
            x = F.interpolate(x, size=(res, res), mode='bilinear', align_corners=False)
            x = (x - _MEAN.to(dev)) /_STD.to(dev)
        else:
            x = F.interpolate(x, size=(res, res), mode='bilinear', align_corners=False)
    return x

@torch.no_grad()
def infer_probs(model, xwins, device):
    assert str(device).startswith("cuda"), "Qualified inference requires CUDA/fp16"
    x = xwins.unsqueeze(0).to(device)
    with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.float16):
        logits = model(x).float()
        assert torch.isfinite(logits).all(), "Nonfinite fp16 logits"
        o = torch.sigmoid(logits)
    return o[0].cpu().numpy()

def rankpct(x):                                   # per-column percentile rank in [0,1]
    order = x.argsort(0).argsort(0).astype(np.float64)
    return order / max(1, (x.shape[0] - 1))

# ============================================================================

# Preprocessing -- verbatim from kprep2/dino_preprocess.py, retargeted to TEST

# ============================================================================

def _make_reader():
    import pydicom, cv2
    from pydicom.pixel_data_handlers.util import apply_modality_lut

    def _hdr(f):
        try:
            h = pydicom.dcmread(f, stop_before_pixels=True)
            iop = getattr(h, 'ImageOrientationPatient', None)
            ipp = getattr(h, 'ImagePositionPatient', None)
            if iop is not None and ipp is not None and len(iop) == 6:
                r = np.array(iop[:3], float); c = np.array(iop[3:], float)
                n = np.cross(r, c); pos = float(np.dot(np.array(ipp, float), n))
            else:
                pos = float(getattr(h, 'InstanceNumber', 0) or 0)
            ps = getattr(h, 'PixelSpacing', None); ps = float(ps[0]) if ps is not None else 0.5
            return (pos, f, ps, True)
        except Exception:
            return (0.0, f, 0.5, False)

    def order_and_meta(sdir):
        # Header reads are latency-bound on Kaggle's mounted competition data (~240k opens on the hidden
        # set); a thread pool hides that latency. Records and sort are unchanged -> identical ordering.
        fs = glob.glob(sdir + "/*.dcm")
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=HDR_THREADS) as ex:
            out = list(ex.map(_hdr, fs))
        recs = [(pos, f, ps) for pos, f, ps, _ in out]; ps_list = [ps for _, _, ps, ok in out if ok]
        recs.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
        med_ps = float(np.median(ps_list)) if ps_list else 0.5
        return [(f, ps) for _, f, ps in recs], med_ps

    def read_px(f):
        d = pydicom.dcmread(f)
        a = apply_modality_lut(d.pixel_array, d).astype(np.float32)
        if str(getattr(d, 'PhotometricInterpretation', '')) == 'MONOCHROME1':
            a = a.max() - a
        return a

    def mm_crop_resize(a, ps):
        h, w = a.shape; cpx = int(round(CROP_MM / max(ps, 1e-3)))
        cpx = min(cpx, min(h, w)); y0 = (h - cpx) // 2; x0 = (w - cpx) // 2
        a = a[y0:y0 + cpx, x0:x0 + cpx]
        return cv2.resize(a, (IMG, IMG), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)

    return order_and_meta, read_px, mm_crop_resize

def _pick_series_for_slot(rows, plane, fluid, used):
    cands = [r for r in rows if r['Anatomical_Plane'] == plane and r['SeriesInstanceUID'] not in used]
    if fluid in (0, 1):
        pref = [r for r in cands if int(r.get('Fluid_Sensitive', 0) or 0) == fluid]
        if pref:
            return pref[0]
    return cands[0] if cands else None

def build_study(sid, ser_records, tsdir, reader):
    order_and_meta, read_px, mm_crop_resize = reader
    rows = ser_records.get(sid, [])
    vol = np.zeros((MAXS, IMG, IMG), np.uint8); idx = 0; used = set()
    for plane, fluid, k in SLOTS:
        r = _pick_series_for_slot(rows, plane, fluid, used)
        if r is None:
            idx += k; continue
        used.add(r['SeriesInstanceUID'])
        files, med_ps = order_and_meta(f"{tsdir}/{sid}/{r['SeriesInstanceUID']}")
        if not files:
            idx += k; continue
        # wide span: the collateral ligaments and lateral meniscus live in the
        # peripheral slices the old 0.15-0.85 crop threw away. Must match the corpus
        # the weights were trained on (build_corpus80.py, SPAN).
        n = len(files); lo, hi = int(n *0.02), int(n* 0.98) - 1; hi = max(hi, lo)
        picks = np.linspace(lo, hi, k).round().astype(int) if n > 1 else [0] *k
        sel = [files[min(p, n - 1)] for p in picks]
        def _px(fp_ps):
            fp, ps = fp_ps
            try: return read_px(fp), ps
            except Exception: return None, med_ps
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=PX_THREADS) as ex:
            got = list(ex.map(_px, sel))                    # order preserved
        arrs = [a for a, _in got]; pss = [ps for_, ps in got]
        valid = [a for a in arrs if a is not None]
        if valid:
            allpx = np.concatenate([a.ravel() for a in valid])
            loq, hiq = np.percentile(allpx, [2.0, 98.0])
        else:
            loq, hiq = 0.0, 1.0
        for a, ps in zip(arrs, pss):
            if idx >= MAXS: break
            if a is None: idx += 1; continue
            aw = np.clip((a - loq) / (hiq - loq + 1e-6), 0, 1)
            aw = mm_crop_resize(aw, ps if ps > 0 else med_ps)
            vol[idx] = (aw* 255).astype(np.uint8); idx += 1
        if idx >= MAXS: break
    mask = (vol.reshape(MAXS, -1).sum(1) > 0).astype(np.uint8)
    return vol, mask

# ---- parallel preprocessing helpers (module level so they pickle under fork) ----

_SER = None;_TSDIR = None; _READER = None
def_pre_init():
    global_READER
    _READER =_load_fastread().make_builder(FASTREAD_VARIANT, IMG, SLOTS, hdr_threads=FR_HDR, px_threads=FR_PX)
def_pre_one(sid):
    try:
        vol, mask =_READER(sid, _SER,_TSDIR)
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"  [pre] {sid[:16]} FAILED ({type(e).__name__}: {e})", flush=True)
        vol = np.zeros((MAXS, IMG, IMG), np.uint8); mask = np.zeros(MAXS, np.uint8)
    return sid, vol, mask

# ============================================================================

# Test-root discovery + weights + main

# ============================================================================

def find_test_root():
    cands = ["/kaggle/input/competitions/rsna-knee-abnormality-detection",
             "/kaggle/input/rsna-knee-abnormality-detection"]
    for b in cands:
        if os.path.exists(b + "/test.csv"):
            return b
    for d, _, f in os.walk("/kaggle/input"):
        if "test.csv" in f and (os.path.isdir(d + "/test_series") or os.path.isdir(d + "/test_images")):
            return d
    for d,_, f in os.walk("/kaggle/input"):
        if "test.csv" in f:
            return d
    raise RuntimeError("no test root under /kaggle/input")

def find_weight_file(fname):
    # direct dataset mounts first; NEVER recursive-glob the competitions DICOM tree.
    direct = [f"/kaggle/input/raptor-knee-arms/{fname}",
              f"/kaggle/input/raptor-knee-arms/1/{fname}",
              f"/kaggle/input/raptor-cnn336/{fname}"]
    for p in direct:
        if os.path.exists(p):
            return p
    for d in sorted(glob.glob("/kaggle/input/*/")):
        # skip the competition data wherever Kaggle mounts it (/kaggle/input/competitions/<slug> OR /kaggle/input/<slug>):
        # recursively globbing its DICOM tree hung a commit run for 20+ min (2026-09-26)
        if "competition" in d.lower() or "abnormality-detection" in d or any(
                os.path.exists(os.path.join(d, x)) for x in ("test_series", "train_images", "train.csv", "test.csv")):
            continue
        hits = glob.glob(os.path.join(d, "**", fname), recursive=True)
        if hits:
            return hits[0]
    raise RuntimeError(f"{fname} not found under /kaggle/input")

def main():
    import pandas as pd
    t0 = time.time()
    dev = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
    ngpu = torch.cuda.device_count()
    print(f"device {dev} | gpus {ngpu} | torch {torch.__version__}", flush=True)

    ROOT = find_test_root()
    tsdir = ROOT + "/test_series"
    if not os.path.isdir(tsdir):
        tsdir = ROOT + "/test_images"
    print("test root:", ROOT, "| series dir:", tsdir, flush=True)

    test = pd.read_csv(ROOT + "/test.csv"); test["StudyInstanceUID"] = test["StudyInstanceUID"].astype(str)
    test_ids = test["StudyInstanceUID"].tolist()
    tser = pd.read_csv(ROOT + "/test_series.csv")
    tser["StudyInstanceUID"] = tser["StudyInstanceUID"].astype(str)
    tser["SeriesInstanceUID"] = tser["SeriesInstanceUID"].astype(str)
    SER = {k: v.to_dict("records") for k, v in tser.groupby("StudyInstanceUID")}
    print(f"test studies {len(test_ids)} | test series {len(tser)}", flush=True)

    sub_cols = ["StudyInstanceUID"] + LAB
    ssub = os.path.join(ROOT, "sample_submission.csv")
    if os.path.exists(ssub):
        sub_cols = list(pd.read_csv(ssub, nrows=1).columns)

    N = len(test_ids); A = len(ARMS)
    arm_probs = [np.full((N, len(LAB)), 0.5, np.float32) for _ in range(A)]
    pos = {sid: i for i, sid in enumerate(test_ids)}

    # ---- 2. GPU consumers: one thread per GPU, every arm loaded on every GPU (per-study batch of 1,
    #         so the math is identical to the sequential kernel; only the wall-clock changes) ----
    xla = None
    try:
        import torch_xla; import torch_xla.core.xla_model as xm
        dev = torch_xla.device(); xla = xm
        print("device: XLA/TPU", dev, flush=True)
    except Exception as e:
        print("XLA unavailable:", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:80], "| device", dev, flush=True)
    devs = [f"cuda:{g}" for g in range(ngpu)] if (xla is None and ngpu > 0) else [dev]
    models = {}
    for d in devs:
        models[d] = []
        for a, arm in enumerate(ARMS):
            model, res = load_model(find_weight_file(arm["file"]), arm["arch"], arm["res"], d)
            models[d].append((model, res))
        print(f"[gpu {d}] loaded {A} arm(s) | {time.time()-t0:.0f}s", flush=True)
    q = queue.Queue(maxsize=QUEUE_MAX); done_n = [0]; lock = threading.Lock()

    def consumer(d):
        while True:
            item = q.get()
            if item is None: q.task_done(); return
            sid, vol, mask = item; i = pos[sid]
            try:
                for a, (model, res) in enumerate(models[d]):
                    xw = eval_windows(vol, mask, k=K_EVAL, res=res, norm=NORM) if xla is not None else gpu_windows(vol, mask, K_EVAL, res, d)
                    assert xla is None, "Anatomical L/R TTA requires CUDA/fp16"
                    xm = gpu_windows(vol, mask, K_EVAL, res, d, norm=NORM, mirror=True)
                    arm_probs[a][i] = infer_pair_probs(model, xw, xm, d)
                    del xm
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  [gpu {d}] study {i} {sid[:16]} FALLBACK ({type(e).__name__}: {e})", flush=True)
            with lock:
                done_n[0] += 1
                if done_n[0] % 200 == 0 or done_n[0] == N:
                    print(f"  [gpu] {done_n[0]}/{N} | {time.time()-t0:.0f}s", flush=True)
            q.task_done()

    threads = [threading.Thread(target=consumer, args=(d,), daemon=True) for d in devs]
    for th in threads: th.start()

    # ---- 3. prep every study ONCE in a process pool and stream it to the GPUs as it lands ----
    import multiprocessing as mp
    global _SER, _TSDIR, _READER
    _SER, _TSDIR = SER, tsdir
    nproc = max(4, min(96, (os.cpu_count() or 8) - 8))
    t_pre = time.time()
    with mp.get_context("fork").Pool(nproc, initializer=_pre_init) as pool:
        for i, (sid, vol, mask) in enumerate(pool.imap_unordered(_pre_one, test_ids, chunksize=2)):
            q.put((sid, vol, mask))
            if (i + 1) % 200 == 0 or i + 1 == N:
                print(f"  [pre] {i+1}/{N} | {time.time()-t0:.0f}s", flush=True)
    print(f"[pre] done: {N} studies in {time.time()-t_pre:.0f}s (procs {nproc} x fastread {FASTREAD_VARIANT} {FR_HDR}/{FR_PX} threads)", flush=True)
    for _ in devs: q.put(None)
    q.join()
    for th in threads: th.join()
    print(f"[gpu] done: {done_n[0]}/{N} studies on {len(devs)} device(s) | {time.time()-t0:.0f}s", flush=True)
    _raw = pd.DataFrame(arm_probs[0].astype(np.float32), columns=LAB)   # [blend] raw probabilities; a failed study stays 0.5
    _raw.insert(0, "StudyInstanceUID", test_ids)
    _raw.to_csv("/kaggle/working/raptor_raw.csv", index=False)
    for d in devs:
        for m, _ in models[d]: del m
    models.clear(); gc.collect()

    # WEIGHTED rank-mean blend across the test set, per finding (the offline recipe).
    # Weights come from ARMS[*]["w"] and are normalised here, so dropping/adding an arm can
    # never silently change the scale. Falls back to equal weights if none are declared.
    _w = np.array([float(a.get("w", 1.0)) for a in ARMS], dtype=np.float64)
    _w = _w / _w.sum()
    print(f"[blend] weighted rank-mean w={dict(zip([a['file'] for a in ARMS], _w.round(4)))}", flush=True)
    ranks = np.tensordot(_w, np.stack([rankpct(np.clip(p, 0, 1)) for p in arm_probs]),
                         axes=(0, 0))                                          # (N,12) in [0,1]
    if not np.isfinite(ranks).all():
        ranks[~np.isfinite(ranks)] = 0.5

    sub = pd.DataFrame(ranks.astype(np.float32), columns=LAB)
    sub.insert(0, "StudyInstanceUID", test_ids)
    sub = sub[sub_cols]
    assert list(sub.columns) == sub_cols, "column order drift"
    assert sub["StudyInstanceUID"].tolist() == test_ids, "row identity drift"
    assert np.isfinite(sub[LAB].values).all()
    out = "/kaggle/working/raptor_sub.csv"   # [blend] Raptor stage output; the blend cell writes submission.csv
    sub.to_csv(out, index=False)
    print("wrote", out, "|", len(sub), "rows x", len(sub.columns), "cols", flush=True)
    print(sub.head().to_string(index=False), flush=True)
    print(f"DONE {time.time()-t0:.0f}s", flush=True)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

RSNA Knee Abnormality Detection
Create a model that can detect knee abnormalities based on multimodal imaging data

This competition requires identity verification
To submit to this competition, you'll need to verify your identity. Learn More

Verify now
Overview
A single knee scan can reveal a dozen different problems. In this competition, you are tasked to build machine learning models that detect a defined set of clinically important abnormalities on knee MRI examinations.

Start

2 months ago
Close

11 days to go
Merger & Entry
Description
The knee is the most commonly injured and imaged joint in the body. Osteoarthritis alone affects an estimated 654 million people worldwide, while acute knee injuries account for 15 to 40 percent of all sports-related trauma. MRIs show clinicians ligaments, cartilage, menisci, and bone in detail, without exposing patients to radiation.

Reading those scans isn’t always straightforward. ACL and MCL tears, meniscal damage, cartilage loss, fractures, and other abnormalities can be subtle, and radiologists don’t always interpret them the same way. Access to musculoskeletal radiologists is also limited, especially outside major medical centers, leading to delays and inconsistent diagnoses.

In this competition, you will develop multimodal machine learning models to detect twelve clinically important knee abnormalities. You'll work with the first RSNA AI Challenge dataset that pairs every imaging study with its original radiology report, enabling your models to learn from both visual scans and written diagnostic text.

High-performing models can act as robust decision support tools, delivering the accuracy, consistency, and speed needed to elevate expert-level knee MRI interpretation and improve care across disparate clinic settings.

Evaluation
Submissions are evaluated by the average area under the ROC curve between the predicted confidence scores and the observed targets across the twelve targets:

The final score is, in other words, the macro-averaged AUC ROC.

Submission File
For each row in the test set, you must predict a confidence score for each of the twelve target labels. The file should contain a header and have the following format:

StudyInstanceUID,ACL,MCL,Medial Meniscus,Lateral Meniscus,Medial OA,Lateral OA,PF OA,Effusion,Synovitis,Baker's,Contusion,Fracture
<uid_1>,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5
<uid_2>,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5
...

Efficiency Prize Evaluation
Efficiency Prize
We are hosting a second track that focuses on model efficiency, because highly accurate models are often computationally heavy.

For the Efficiency Prize, we will evaluate submissions on both runtime and predictive performance.

To be eligible for an Efficiency Prize, a submission:

Must be among the submissions selected by a team for the Leaderboard Prize, or else among those submissions automatically selected under the conditions described in the My Submissions tab.
Must be ranked on the Private Leaderboard higher than the sample_submission.csv benchmark.
All submissions meeting these conditions will be considered for the Efficiency Prize. A submission may be eligible for both the Leaderboard Prize and the Efficiency Prize.

An Efficiency Prize will be awarded to eligible submissions according to how they are ranked by the following evaluation metric on the private test data. See the Prizes tab for the prize awarded to each rank. More details may be posted via discussion forum updates.

The Competition named below is a skills-based competition to promote and further the field of data science. You must register via the Competition Website to enter. To enter the Competition, you must agree to these Official Competition Rules, which incorporate by reference the provisions and content of the Competition Website and any Specific Competition Rules herein (collectively, the "Rules"). Please read these Rules carefully before entry to ensure you understand and agree. You further agree that Submission in the Competition constitutes agreement to these Rules. You may not submit to the Competition and are not eligible to receive the prizes associated with this Competition unless you agree to these Rules. These Rules form a binding legal agreement between you and the Competition Sponsor with respect to the Competition. Your competition Submissions must conform to the requirements stated on the Competition Website. Your Submissions will be scored based on the evaluation metric described on the Competition Website. Subject to compliance with the Competition Rules, Prizes, if any, will be awarded to Participants with the best scores, based on the merits of the data science models submitted. See below for the complete Competition Rules. For Hackathon Competitions, your Submissions will be judged by the Competition Sponsor based on the evaluation rubric set forth on the Competition Website (“Evaluation Rubric”). The Prizes, if any, will be awarded to Participants with the highest ranking(s) as determined by the Competition Sponsor based on the Evaluation Rubric.

You cannot sign up to Kaggle from multiple accounts and therefore you cannot enter or submit from multiple accounts.
